Since the 19th century, the [United States government](195149.md) has participated and interfered, both overtly and covertly, in the replacement of many foreign governments. In the latter half of the 19th century, the U.S. government initiated actions for [regime change](154074.md) mainly in [Latin America](56153035.md) and the southwest Pacific, including the [Spanish–American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish%E2%80%93American_War) and [Philippine–American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine%E2%80%93American_War) wars. At the onset of the 20th century, the [United States](3434750.md) shaped or installed governments in many countries around the world, including neighbors [Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Hawaii_as_United_States_territory), [Panama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_involvement_in_Panama), [Honduras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rise_of_United_States_influence_in_Honduras_\(1899%E2%80%931919\)), [Nicaragua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_occupation_of_Nicaragua), [Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Government_of_Veracruz), [Haiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_occupation_of_Haiti), and the [Dominican Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Government_of_Santo_Domingo).

During [World War II](32927.md), the U.S. helped overthrow many [Nazi German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_German) or [Imperial Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_Japanese)-controlled [puppet regimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_War_II_puppet_states). Examples include regimes in the [Philippines](23440.md), [Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korea), [East China](683693.md), and parts of Europe. United States forces, together with the [United Kingdom](31717.md) and [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union), were also instrumental in [collapsing Adolf Hitler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collapse_of_Nazi_Germany)'s government in Germany and [deposing Benito Mussolini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_the_Fascist_regime_in_Italy) in Italy. At the end of World War II, the U.S. government struggled with the Soviet Union for global leadership, influence and security within the context of the [Cold War](325329.md). Under the [Truman administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truman_administration), the U.S. government, ostensibly for fear that [Soviet Communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Communism) would be spread, sometimes with the assistance of the [Soviet's own involvement in regime change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_involvement_in_regime_change), promoted the [domino theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domino_theory), a precedent which later presidents followed. Subsequently, the U.S. expanded the geographic scope of its actions beyond the traditional area of operations; [Central America](6121.md) and the [Caribbean](18956035.md).

Significant operations included the United States and [United Kingdom](31717.md)–planned [1953 Iranian coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1953_Iranian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat), the [1954 Guatemalan coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1954_Guatemalan_coup_d'%C3%A9tat), the 1961 [Bay of Pigs Invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_of_Pigs_Invasion) targeting Cuba, and [support for the overthrow of Sukarno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transition_to_the_New_Order#United_States_assistance_to_Suharto) by General [Suharto](217513.md) in [Indonesia](14579.md). In addition, the U.S. has [interfered in the national elections](52846360.md) of countries, including [Italy](14532.md) in 1948,[^cite_note-1] the Philippines in 1953, [Japan](15573.md) in the 1950s and 1960s,[^cite_note-2][^cite_note-3] [Lebanon](17771.md) in 1957,[^cite_note-4] and [Russia](25391.md) in 1996.[^cite_note-5] According to one study, the U.S. performed at least 81 overt and covert known interventions in foreign elections from 1946 to 2000.[^cite_note-6] According to another study, the U.S. engaged in 64 covert and six overt attempts at regime change during the Cold War.[^cite_note-\:1-7] Following the [dissolution of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union), the United States has led or supported wars to determine the governance of a number of countries. Stated U.S. aims in these conflicts have included fighting the [war on terror](13425800.md), as in the [Afghan War](19666611.md), or removing supposed [weapons of mass destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weapon_of_mass_destruction) (WMDs), as in the [Iraq War](5043324.md). In the 2020s, the [second Donald Trump administration](78298108.md) was [heavily involved in regime change efforts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_imperialism#Trump_administration) using economic pressure and military threats like in the [Greenland crisis](82044223.md) and [2026 Cuban crisis](82348646.md) and removed or killed government officials using military force in the [2026 United States intervention in Venezuela](82021123.md) and the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md).

## Prior to 1887

### 1846–1848: Annexation of Texas and invasion of California

Main articles: [Mexican–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican%E2%80%93American_War) and [Texas annexation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_annexation)

The United States annexed the [Republic of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Texas), at the time considered by [Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centralist_Republic_of_Mexico) to be a rebellious [state of Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coahuila_y_Tejas).[^cite_note-8] During the war with Mexico that ensued, the United States seized [Alta California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alta_California) from [Mexico](3966054.md).[^cite_note-9]

### 1865–1867: Mexico

See also: [Second French intervention in Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_French_intervention_in_Mexico)

While the [American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War) was taking place in the United States, [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_French_Empire) and other countries invaded Mexico to collect debts. France then installed [Habsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Habsburg) prince [Maximilian I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximilian_I_of_Mexico) as the [Emperor of Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emperor_of_Mexico). After the Civil War ended, the United States began supporting the [Liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_Party_\(Mexico\)) forces of [Benito Juárez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benito_Ju%C3%A1rez) (who had been the interim [President of Mexico](24356889.md) since 1858 under the liberal [Constitution of 1857](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_1857) and then elected as president in 1861 before the French invasion) against the forces of Maximilian. The United States began sending and dropping arms into Mexico and many Americans fought alongside Juárez. Eventually, Juárez and the Liberals took back power and executed Maximillian I.[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11][^cite_note-12]

The United States had opposed Maximilian, invoking the [Monroe Doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monroe_Doctrine). Around 1867, shortly afterwards, [Secretary of State](32293.md) [William Seward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_H._Seward) exposited: "The Monroe Doctrine, which eight years ago was merely a theory, is now an irreversible fact."[^cite_note-13]

## 1887–1912: U.S. expansionism and Roosevelt administration

### 1880s

#### 1887–1889: Samoa

Main articles: [Samoan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samoan_Civil_War), [Samoan crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samoan_crisis), and [Second Samoan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Samoan_Civil_War)



Samoa in Oceania

In the 1880s, [Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Samoa) was a monarchy with two rival claimants to the throne: [Malietoa Laupepa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malietoa_Laupepa) and [Mata'afa Iosefo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mata'afa_Iosefo). The [Samoan crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samoan_crisis) was a confrontation between the United States, [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Empire) and the [United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_Great_Britain_and_Ireland) from 1887 to 1889, with the powers backing rival claimants to the throne of the [Samoan Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samoan_Islands) which became the [First Samoan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samoan_Civil_War).[^cite_note-14]

### 1890s

#### 1893: Kingdom of Hawaii

Main articles: [Overthrow of the Hawaiian Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overthrow_of_the_Hawaiian_Kingdom) and [Republic of Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Hawaii)



Hawaii in Oceania

Anti-monarchists, mostly Americans, in [Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaiian_Islands), engineered the [overthrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overthrow_of_the_Hawaiian_Kingdom) of the [Kingdom of Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hawaii). On January 17, 1893, the native monarch, Queen [Lili'uokalani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liliuokalani), was overthrown. Hawaii was initially [reconstituted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Hawaii) as an independent republic, but the ultimate goal of the action was the annexation of the islands to the United States, which was finally accomplished with the [Newlands Resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newlands_Resolution) of 1898.[^cite_note-15]

#### 1899–1902: Philippines

Main articles: [Philippine–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine%E2%80%93American_War) and [History of the Philippines (1898–1946)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Philippines_\(1898%E2%80%931946\))

The successful [Philippine Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Revolution) saw the defeat of the [Spanish Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Empire) and the establishment of the [First Philippine Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Philippine_Republic), ending centuries of [Spanish colonial rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captaincy_General_of_the_Philippines) in the archipelago. The U.S., which had allied with the revolutionaries and emerged victorious in the concurrent [Spanish–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish%E2%80%93American_War), was "granted" the Philippines in the [Treaty of Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_\(1898\)). Wishing to establish its own control over the country, the U.S. engaged in the [Philippine–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine%E2%80%93American_War), the success of which saw the dissolution of the self-governing Philippine Republic and formation of an [Insular Government of the Philippine Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_territory) in 1902. The Philippines became a [self-governing Commonwealth in 1935](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_the_Philippines) and was granted full sovereignty by 1946.

### 1900s

#### 1903: Colombia

Main article: [Secession of Panama from Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secession_of_Panama_from_Colombia)

In September 1903 [Manuel Amador Guerrero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Amador_Guerrero), leader of the movement for Panamanian independence from Colombia, traveled to New York to determine how the United States might support the movement. U.S. President Theodore Roosevelt privately supported the separatist movement, later ordering the warship USS *Nashville* under commander John Hubbard to proceed first to Jamaica, then to Panama.

#### 1903–1925: Honduras

Main article: [Banana Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banana_Wars)



In what became known as the "[Banana Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banana_Wars)", between the end of the [Spanish–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish%E2%80%93American_War) in 1898 and the inception of the [Good Neighbor Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Neighbor_Policy) in 1934, the U.S. staged many military invasions and interventions in [Central America](6121.md) and the [Caribbean](18956035.md).[^cite_note-16] One of these incursions, in 1903, involved regime change rather than regime preservation. The [United States Marine Corps](17349325.md), which most often fought these wars, developed a manual called *[The Strategy and Tactics of Small Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_Wars_Manual)* in 1921 based on its experiences. On occasion, the [Navy](20518076.md) provided [gunfire support](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naval_gunfire_support) and [Army](32087.md) troops were also used. The [United Fruit Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Fruit_Company) and [Standard Fruit Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Fruit_Company) dominated [Honduras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Honduras)' key [banana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banana) export sector and associated land holdings and railways. The U.S. staged invasions and incursions of US troops in 1903 (supporting a coup by [Manuel Bonilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Bonilla)), 1907 (supporting Bonilla against a Nicaraguan-backed coup), 1911 and 1912 (defending the regime of [Miguel R. Davila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_R._Davila) from an uprising), 1919 (peacekeeping during a civil war, and installing the caretaker government of [Francisco Bográn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Bogr%C3%A1n)), 1920 (defending the Bográn regime from a general strike), 1924 (defending the regime of [Rafael López Gutiérrez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_L%C3%B3pez_Guti%C3%A9rrez) from an uprising) and 1925 (defending the elected government of [Miguel Paz Barahona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Paz_Barahona)) to defend US interests.[^cite_note-17]

#### 1906–1909: Cuba

Main article: [Second Occupation of Cuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Occupation_of_Cuba)



After the explosion of the [USS *Maine*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Maine_\(ACR-1\)) the United States declared war on Spain, starting the [Spanish–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish%E2%80%93American_War).[^cite_note-18] The United States invaded and occupied [Spanish-ruled Cuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captaincy_General_of_Cuba) in 1898. Many in the United States did not want to annex Cuba and Congress passed the [Teller Amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teller_Amendment), forbidding annexation. Cuba was occupied by the U.S. and run by military governor [Leonard Wood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonard_Wood) during the [first occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Military_Government_in_Cuba) from 1898 to 1902, after the end of the war. The [Platt Amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Platt_Amendment) was passed later on outlining [Cuba–United States relations](5231073.md). It said the U.S. could intervene anytime against a government that was not approved, forced Cuba to accept U.S. influence, and limited Cuban abilities to make foreign relations.[^cite_note-19] The United States forced Cuba to accept the terms of the Platt Amendment, by putting it into their constitution.[^cite_note-20] After the occupation, Cuba and the U.S. would sign the [Cuban–American Treaty of Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuban%E2%80%93American_Treaty_of_Relations_\(1903\)) in 1903, further agreeing to the terms of the Platt Amendment.[^cite_note-21]

[Tomás Estrada Palma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom%C3%A1s_Estrada_Palma) became the first [President of Cuba](516041.md) after the U.S. withdrew. He was a member of the [Republican Party of Havana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Party_of_Havana). He was re-elected in 1905 unopposed; however, the [Liberals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_Party_of_Cuba) accused him of electoral fraud. Fighting began between the Liberals and Republicans. Due to the tensions he resigned on September 28, 1906, and his government collapsed soon afterwards. U.S. Secretary of war [William Howard Taft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Howard_Taft) invoked the Platt Amendment and the 1903 treaty, under approval of President [Theodore Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodore_Roosevelt), invading the country, and occupying it. The country would be governed by [Charles Edward Magoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Edward_Magoon) during the occupation. They oversaw the election of [José Miguel Gómez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Miguel_G%C3%B3mez) in 1909, and afterwards withdrew from the country.[^cite_note-22]

#### 1909–1910: Nicaragua

See also: [United States occupation of Nicaragua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_occupation_of_Nicaragua)

Governor [Juan José Estrada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Jos%C3%A9_Estrada), member of the [Conservative Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_\(Nicaragua\)), led a revolt against President [José Santos Zelaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Santos_Zelaya), member of the [Liberal Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_\(Nicaragua\)) reelected in 1906. This became what is known as the [Estrada rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estrada_rebellion?action=edit\&redlink=1). The United States supported the conservative forces because Zelaya had wanted to work with Germany or Japan to build a [new canal through the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicaragua_Canal). The U.S. controlled the [Panama Canal](24849.md) and did not want competition from another country outside of the Americas. Thomas P Moffat, a US council in [Bluefields, Nicaragua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bluefields),[^cite_note-Musicant-23] would give overt support, in conflict with the US trying to only give covert support. Direct intervention would be pushed by the secretary of state [Philander C. Knox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philander_C._Knox). Two Americans were executed by Zelaya for their participation with the conservatives. Seeing an opportunity the United States became directly involved in the rebellion and sent in troops, which landed on the [Mosquito Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mosquito_Coast). On December 14, 1909, Zelaya was forced to resign under diplomatic pressure from America and fled Nicaragua. Before Zelaya fled, he, along with the liberal assembly, chose [José Madriz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Madriz) to lead Nicaragua. The U.S. refused to recognize Madriz. The conservatives eventually beat back the liberals and forced Madriz to resign. Estrada then became the president. [Thomas Cleland Dawson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Cleland_Dawson) was sent as a special agent to the country and determined that any election held would bring the liberals into power, so had Estrada set up a constituent assembly to elect him instead. In August 1910 Estrada became [President of Nicaragua](302862.md) under U.S. recognition, agreeing to certain conditions from the U.S. After the intervention, the U.S. and Nicaragua signed a treaty on June 6, 1911.[^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26]

## 1912–1941: Wilson administration, World War I and interwar period

### 1910s

#### 1912–1933: Nicaragua

See also: [United States occupation of Nicaragua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_occupation_of_Nicaragua)



The [Taft administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_William_Howard_Taft) sent troops into Nicaragua and occupied the country. When the [Wilson administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Woodrow_Wilson) came into power, they extended the stay and took complete financial and governmental control of the country, leaving a heavily armed legation. U.S. president [Calvin Coolidge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calvin_Coolidge) removed troops from the country, leaving a legation and Adolfo Diaz in charge of the country. Rebels ended up capturing the town with the legation and Diaz requested troops came back, which they did a few months after leaving. The U.S. government fought against rebels led by [Augusto Cesar Sandino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augusto_C%C3%A9sar_Sandino). [Franklin D. Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt) pulled out because the U.S. could no longer afford to keep troops in the country due to the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression). The second intervention in Nicaragua would become one of the longest wars in United States history. The United States left the [Somoza family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somoza_family) in charge, who killed Sandino in 1934.[^cite_note-27]

#### 1913: Mexico

Main article: [Military Government of Veracruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Government_of_Veracruz)

[Henry Lane Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Lane_Wilson), U.S. ambassador to Mexico under [William Howard Taft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Howard_Taft), actively supported the [Ten Tragic Days](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ten_Tragic_Days) coup which overthrew the democratically elected president, [Francisco I. Madero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_I._Madero). Soon after taking office, U.S. president [Woodrow Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodrow_Wilson) dismissed the ambassador and refused to recognize the Mexican government of [Victoriano Huerta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoriano_Huerta), who had seized power in the coup. This led to the [United States occupation of Veracruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_occupation_of_Veracruz) in 1914 and continued instability in Mexico.

#### 1915–1934: Haiti

Main article: [United States occupation of Haiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_occupation_of_Haiti)



The U.S. [occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_occupation_of_Haiti) [Haiti](13373.md) from 1915 to 1934. U.S.-based banks had lent money to Haiti and the banks requested U.S. government intervention. In an example of "[gunboat diplomacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gunboat_diplomacy)", the U.S. sent its navy to intimidate to get its way.[^cite_note-28] Eventually, in 1917, the U.S. installed a new government and dictated the terms of a new Haitian [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution) of 1917 that instituted changes that included an end to the prior ban on land ownership by non-Haitians. The [Cacos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cacos_\(military_group\)) were originally armed militias of formerly enslaved persons who rebelled and took control of mountainous areas following the [Haitian Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haitian_Revolution) in 1804. Such groups fought a guerrilla war against the U.S. occupation in what were known as the "[Caco Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caco_Wars)".[^cite_note-29]

#### 1916–1924: Dominican Republic

Main article: [Military Government of Santo Domingo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Government_of_Santo_Domingo)



[U.S. marines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._marines) invaded the [Dominican Republic](8060.md) and [occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_occupation_of_the_Dominican_Republic_\(1916%E2%80%9324\)) it from 1916 to 1924, and this was preceded by US military interventions in 1903, 1904, and 1914. The [US Navy](20518076.md) installed its personnel in all key positions in government and controlled the [Dominican military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_Forces_of_the_Dominican_Republic) and police.[^cite_note-Proceedings-30] Within a couple of days, President [Juan Isidro Jimenes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Isidro_Jimenes_Pereyra) resigned.[^cite_note-31]

#### 1918–1920: Russia

Main article: [Allied intervention in the Russian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allied_intervention_in_the_Russian_Civil_War)



In 1918, the U.S. military took part in the [Allied intervention in the Russian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allied_intervention_in_the_Russian_Civil_War) to support the [White movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_movement) and overthrow the [Bolsheviks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolsheviks).[^cite_note-32] [President Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_Wilson) [agreed to send](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_and_the_Russian_Revolution) 5,000 [United States Army](32087.md) troops in the campaign. This force, which became known as the "American North Russia Expeditionary Force"[^cite_note-E.M._Halliday,_2000_p._44-33] (a.k.a. the [Polar Bear Expedition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polar_Bear_Expedition)) launched the [North Russia Campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Russia_Campaign) from [Arkhangelsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arkhangelsk), while another 8,000 soldiers, organised as the [American Expeditionary Force Siberia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Expeditionary_Force_Siberia),[^cite_note-Robert_L._Willett_pp._166-34] launched the [Siberia intervention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allied_Intervention_in_Siberia) from [Vladivostok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladivostok).[^cite_note-beyer-35] The forces were withdrawn in 1920.[^cite_note-HistoryRussia-36]

## 1941–1945: World War II and aftermath

Main article: [Military history of the United States during World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_the_United_States_during_World_War_II)

### 1941–1952: Japan

Main article: [Occupation of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupation_of_Japan)



Representatives of the Empire of Japan stand aboard [USS *Missouri*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Missouri_\(BB-63\)) prior to signing of the [Instrument of Surrender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Instrument_of_Surrender)

In December 1941, the US joined the [Allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II) in [war](32927.md) against the [Empire of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan), a monarchy. After the Allied victory, Japan was occupied by Allied forces under the command of American general [Douglas MacArthur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_MacArthur). In 1946, the [Japanese Diet](163847.md) ratified a new [Constitution of Japan](62699.md) that followed closely a 'model copy' prepared by MacArthur's command,[^cite_note-37] and was promulgated as an amendment to the old [Prussian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prussia)-style [Meiji Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meiji_Constitution). The constitution renounced aggressive war and was accompanied by liberalization of many areas of Japanese life. While liberalizing life for most Japanese, the Allies [tried many Japanese war criminals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Military_Tribunal_for_the_Far_East) and executed some, while granting amnesty to the family of emperor [Hirohito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hirohito).[^cite_note-Dower-38] The occupation was ended by the [Treaty of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_San_Francisco).[^cite_note-Dower-38]

Following the [United States invasion of Okinawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Okinawa) during the [Pacific War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_War), the U.S. installed the [United States Military Government of the Ryukyu Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Military_Government_of_the_Ryukyu_Islands). Pursuant to a treaty with the Japanese government (Message of Emperor), in 1950 the [United States Civil Administration of the Ryukyu Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Civil_Administration_of_the_Ryukyu_Islands) took over and ruled [Okinawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Okinawa_Island) and the rest of the [Ryukyu Islands](8458939.md) until 1972. During this "trusteeship rule", the U.S. built numerous military bases, including bases that operated [nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_weapons_of_the_United_States). U.S. rule was opposed by many local residents, creating the [Ryukyu independence movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryukyu_independence_movement) that struggled against U.S. rule.[^cite_note-nationalarchives-39]

### 1941–1949: Germany

Main articles: [Western Allied invasion of Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Allied_invasion_of_Germany), [Allied-occupied Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allied-occupied_Germany), and [Denazification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denazification)

In December 1941, the United States joined the Allied campaign against [Nazi Germany](21212.md), a [fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascist) dictatorship. The US took part in the [Allied occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allied-occupied_Germany) and [Denazification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denazification) of the [Western portion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bizone) of [Germany](11867.md). Former [Nazis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism) were subjected to varying levels of punishment, depending on how the US assessed their levels of guilt. At the end of 1947, for example, the Allies held 90,000 Nazis in [detention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detention_\(imprisonment\)); another 1,900,000 were forbidden to work as anything but manual laborers.[^cite_note-40] As Germans took more and more responsibility for Germany, they pushed for an end to the denazification process, and the Americans allowed this. In 1949, the [Federal Republic of Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Germany), a [parliamentary democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_democracy) in West Germany was formed.[^cite_note-41] The main denazification process came to an end with amnesty laws passed in 1951.[^cite_note-42]

### 1941–1946: Italy

Main articles: [Italian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Civil_War) and [Liberation of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_of_Italy)

In July–August 1943, the US participated in the [Allied invasion of Sicily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allied_invasion_of_Sicily), spearheaded by the [U.S. Seventh Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seventh_United_States_Army), under [Lieutenant General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lieutenant_general_\(United_States\)) [George S. Patton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_S._Patton), in which over 2,000 US servicemen were killed,[^cite_note-43] initiating the [Italian Campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Campaign_\(World_War_II\)) which conquered Italy from the fascist regime of [Benito Mussolini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benito_Mussolini) and its Nazi German allies. Mussolini was arrested by order of King [Victor Emmanuel III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Emmanuel_III), provoking a [civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Civil_War). The king appointed [Pietro Badoglio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pietro_Badoglio) as new [Prime Minister](273741.md). Badoglio stripped away the final elements of Fascist rule by banning the [National Fascist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Fascist_Party), then signed an [armistice with the Allied armed forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armistice_between_Italy_and_Allied_armed_forces). The [Royal Italian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Italian_Army) outside of the peninsula itself collapsed; its occupied and annexed territories fell under [German control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Achse). Italy [capitulated to the Allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armistice_of_Cassibile) on 3 September 1943. The northern half of the country was occupied by the Germans with help from [Italian fascists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_fascism) and made a [collaborationist puppet state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Social_Republic), while the south was governed by monarchist forces, which fought for the Allied cause as the [Italian Co-Belligerent Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Co-Belligerent_Army).[^cite_note-44]

### 1944–1946: France

Main articles: [Liberation of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_of_France), [Operation Goodwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Goodwood), and [Operation Cobra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Cobra)

See also: [Free France § Liberation of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_France#Liberation_of_France)



General [Charles de Gaulle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_de_Gaulle) and his entourage proudly stroll down the [Champs-Élysées](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champs-%C3%89lys%C3%A9es) to [Notre-Dame Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Notre-Dame_de_Paris) for a [Te Deum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Te_Deum) ceremony following Paris's liberation on 25 August 1944.

British, Canadian, and United States forces were critical participants in [Operation Goodwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Goodwood) and [Operation Cobra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Cobra), leading to a military breakout that ended the [Nazi occupation of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_military_administration_in_occupied_France_during_World_War_II). The actual [Liberation of Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_of_Paris) was accomplished by French forces. The French formed the [Provisional Government of the French Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provisional_Government_of_the_French_Republic) in 1944, leading to the formation of the [French Fourth Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Fourth_Republic) in 1946. The liberation of France is celebrated regularly up to the present day.[^cite_note-45][^cite_note-46]

### 1944–1945: Belgium

Main article: [Liberation of Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_of_Belgium)



American troops during the Battle of the Bulge

In the wake of the 1940 invasion, Germany established the [Reichskommissariat of Belgium and Northern France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reichskommissariat_of_Belgium_and_Northern_France) to govern Belgium. United States, Canadian, British, and other Allied forces ended the [Nazi occupation of most of Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_occupation_of_Belgium_during_World_War_II) in September 1944. The [Belgian Government in Exile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_Government_in_Exile) under Prime Minister [Hubert Pierlot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Pierlot) returned on 8 September.[^cite_note-47] In December, American forces suffered over 80,000 casualties defending Belgium from a German counterattack in the [Battle of the Bulge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_the_Bulge). By February 1945, all of Belgium was in Allied hands.[^cite_note-48] The year 1945 was chaotic. Pierlot resigned, and [Achille Van Acker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achille_Van_Acker) of the [Belgian Socialist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_Socialist_Party) formed a new government. There were riots over the [Royal Question](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Question)—the return of King [Leopold III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopold_III_of_Belgium). Although the war continued, Belgians were again in control of their own country.[^cite_note-49]

### 1944–1945: Netherlands

Main articles: [Operation Market Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Market_Garden) and [Operation Plunder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Plunder)

During the [Nazi occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands_in_World_War_II), the Netherlands was governed by the [Reichskommissariat Niederlande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reichskommissariat_Niederlande), headed by [Arthur Seyss-Inquart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Seyss-Inquart). British, Canadian, and American forces liberated portions of the Netherlands in September 1944; however, after the failure of [Operation Market Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Market_Garden), the liberation of the largest cities had to wait until the last weeks of the [European theatre of World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_theatre_of_World_War_II). British and American forces [crossed the Rhine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Plunder) on 23 March 1945; Canadian forces in their wake then entered the Netherlands from the east. The remaining German forces in the Netherlands surrendered on 5 May, which is celebrated as [Liberation Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Day_\(Netherlands\)) in the Netherlands. [Queen Wilhelmina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelmina_of_the_Netherlands) returned on 2 May; [elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1946_Dutch_general_election) were held in 1946, leading to a new government headed by Prime Minister [Louis Beel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Beel).[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-51]

### 1944–1945: Philippines

Main articles: [Philippines Campaign (1944–1945)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines_Campaign_\(1944%E2%80%931945\)) and [Commonwealth of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_the_Philippines)



General [Douglas MacArthur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_MacArthur), President [Osmeña](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Osme%C3%B1a) and staff land at [Palo, Leyte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palo,_Leyte) on October 20, 1944

United States landings in 1944 ended the [Japanese occupation of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_occupation_of_the_Philippines).[^cite_note-52] After the Japanese were defeated and the puppet regime that was controlling the [Second Philippine Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Philippine_Republic) was overthrown, the United States fulfilled a promise by granting independence to the Philippines. [Sergio Osmeña](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Osme%C3%B1a) formed the government of the restored [Commonwealth of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_the_Philippines), overseeing democratic transition to the fully sovereign [Third Philippine Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Philippine_Republic) in 1946.[^cite_note-pinas-53]

### 1945–1955: Austria

Main article: [Allied-occupied Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allied-occupied_Austria)

Austria was annexed to Germany in the 1938 [Anschluss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anschluss). As German citizens, many Austrians fought on the side of Germany during World War II. After the Allied victory, the Allies treated Austria as a victim of Nazi aggression, rather than as a perpetrator. The United States [Marshall Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marshall_Plan) provided aid.[^cite_note-54] The 1955 [Austrian State Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austrian_State_Treaty) re-established Austria as a free, democratic, and sovereign state. It was signed by representatives of the United States, the Soviet Union, the United Kingdom, and France. It provided for the withdrawal of all occupying troops and guaranteed Austrian neutrality in the Cold War.[^cite_note-55]

## 1945–1991: Cold War

### 1940s

#### 1945–1948: South Korea

Main articles: [United States Army Military Government in Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Army_Military_Government_in_Korea), [First Republic of Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Republic_of_Korea), and [Syngman Rhee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syngman_Rhee)

The [Empire of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan) surrendered to the United States in August 1945, ending the [Japanese rule of Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korea_under_Japanese_rule). Under the leadership of [Lyuh Woon-Hyung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyuh_Woon-Hyung) [People's Committees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Committee_\(postwar_Korea\)) throughout [Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korea) formed to coordinate transition to Korean independence. On August 28, 1945, these committees formed the temporary national government of Korea, naming it the [People's Republic of Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Republic_of_Korea) (PRK) a couple of weeks later.[^cite_note-56][^cite_note-57] On September 8, 1945, the United States government landed forces in Korea and thereafter established the [United States Army Military Government in Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Army_Military_Government_in_Korea) (USAMGK) to govern Korea south of the [38th parallel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/38th_parallel_north). The USAMGK outlawed the PRK committees which by that point had been co-opted by the communists.[^cite_note-58][^cite_note-59]

In May 1948, [Syngman Rhee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syngman_Rhee), who had previously lived in the United States, won the [1948 South Korean presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_South_Korean_presidential_election), which had been boycotted by most other politicians and in which voting was limited to property owners and tax payers or, in smaller towns, to town elders voting for everyone else.[^cite_note-60][^cite_note-61] Syngman Rhee, backed by the U.S. government, set up authoritarian rule that coordinated closely with the business sector and lasted until Rhee's overthrow in 1961, which led to a similarly authoritarian regime that would last ultimately until the late 1980s.[^cite_note-62]

#### 1947–1949: Greece

Main article: [Greek Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Civil_War)



[Greece](12108.md) had been under [Axis occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axis_occupation_of_Greece) since 1941. Its [government-in-exile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_government-in-exile), unelected and loyal to [King George II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_II_of_Greece), was based in [Cairo](6293.md). By the Summer of 1944, Communist guerrillas, then known as the [Greek People's Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_People's_Liberation_Army) (ELAS), who had been armed by the Western powers, exploiting the gradual collapse of the Axis, claimed to have liberated nearly all of Greece outside of [Athens](1216.md) from Axis occupation, while also attacking and defeating rival non-Communist partisan groups, forming a rival unelected government, the [Political Committee of National Liberation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_Committee_of_National_Liberation). On 12 August 1944, German forces retreated from the Athens area two days ahead of British landings there, ending the occupation.[^cite_note-63]

The [British Armed Forces](31730.md) together with [Greek](12108.md) forces under control of the Greek government (now a government of national unity led by [Konstantinos Tsaldaris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstantinos_Tsaldaris), elected in the [1946 Greek legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1946_Greek_legislative_election) boycotted by the [Communist Party of Greece](356444.md)) then fought for control of the country in the [Greek Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Civil_War) against the Communists, who at that time were self-proclaimed as the [Democratic Army of Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Army_of_Greece) (DSE). By early 1947, the British government could no longer afford the huge cost of financing the war against DSE, and pursuant to the October 1944 [Percentages Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Percentages_Agreement) between [Winston Churchill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Churchill) and [Joseph Stalin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Stalin), Greece was to remain part of the Western [sphere of influence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sphere_of_influence). Accordingly, the British requested the U.S. government to step in and the U.S. flooded the country with military equipment, military advisers and weapons.[^cite_note-DM-64]<sup>: 553–554 </sup>[^cite_note-JTP-65]<sup>: 129 </sup>[^cite_note-66][^cite_note-67] With increased [U.S. military aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_military_aid), by September 1949 the government eventually won, fully restoring the [Kingdom of Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Greece).[^cite_note-Herring-68]<sup>: 616–617 </sup>

#### 1948: Costa Rica

Main article: [Costa Rican Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costa_Rican_Civil_War)

[Christian socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_socialist) medic [Rafael Ángel Calderón Guardia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_%C3%81ngel_Calder%C3%B3n_Guardia) of the [National Republican Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Republican_Party_\(Costa_Rica\)) [was elected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1944_Costa_Rican_general_election) in 1944 and promoted general [social reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_reform).[^cite_note-Lorenz2-69] In the [1948 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Costa_Rican_general_election), the opposition won the presidency but lost the Congress. This prompted the Congress to annul the results of the presidential election but not the results of the congressional election; on the same day as the annulment, the leader of the opposition campaign was assassinated.[^cite_note-70] These events led to the short-lived [Costa Rican Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costa_Rican_Civil_War) of 1948, in which the US supported the opposition, and [Somoza-run Nicaragua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somoza_family) supported Calderón. The war ended Calderón's government and led to the short de facto rule of 18 months by [José Figueres Ferrer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Figueres_Ferrer).[^cite_note-Lorenz2-69] However, Figueres also held some left-leaning ideas and continued the process of social reform.[^cite_note-bailey2-71] After the war, democracy was quickly restored and a [two-party system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-party_system) encompassed by the parties of the [Calderonistas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calderonistas) and [Figueristas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Figueristas) developed in the country for nearly 60 years.[^cite_note-bailey2-71]

#### 1949–1953: Albania

See also: [Albanian Subversion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Subversion)



[Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Socialist_Republic_of_Albania) was in chaos after [World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II_in_Albania) and the country was not as focused on peacetime conferences in comparison to other European nations, while having suffered high casualties.[^cite_note-72] It was threatened by its larger neighbors with annexation. After [Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia) [dropped out](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tito%E2%80%93Stalin_split) of the [Eastern Bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Bloc), the small country of Albania was geographically isolated from the rest of the Eastern Bloc. The United States and United Kingdom took advantage of the situation and recruited [anti-communist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-communist) Albanians who had fled after the USSR invaded. The US and UK formed the [Free Albania National Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Albania_National_Committee), made up of many of the emigres. Recruited Albanians were trained by the U.S. and U.K. and infiltrated the country multiple times. Eventually, the operation was found out and many of the agents fled, were executed, or were tried. The operation would become a failure. The operation was declassified in 2006, due to the [Nazi War Crimes Disclosure Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_War_Crimes_and_Japanese_Imperial_Government_Records_Interagency_Working_Group) and is now available in the National Archives.[^cite_note-73][^cite_note-74]



#### 1949: Syria

Main article: [March 1949 Syrian coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_1949_Syrian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat)

The government of [Shukri al-Quwatli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shukri_al-Quwatli), reelected in 1948, was [overthrown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_1949_Syrian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat) by a junta led by the [Syrian Army](3423927.md) chief of staff at the time, [Husni al-Za'im](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Husni_al-Za'im), who became President of Syria on April 11, 1949. Za'im had extensive connections to CIA operatives,[^cite_note-75] and promptly approved the construction of America's TAPLINE oil pipeline in Syria, considered an important Cold War project and blocked by Quwatly's pre-coup government.[^cite_note-Little-76] The actual extent of U.S. involvement in the coup remains unclear.[^cite_note-77][^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79]

### 1950s



#### 1950–1953: Burma and China

See also: [Kuomintang in Burma § CIA assistance and opium trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuomintang_in_Burma#CIA_assistance_and_opium_trade)

The [Chinese Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Civil_War) had recently ended, with the [Communists](7175.md) winning and the [Nationalists](16903.md) losing. The nationalists retreated to areas such as [Taiwan](25734.md) and north [Burma](19457.md).[^cite_note-80] Operation Paper began in late 1950,[^cite_note-Scott_2010-81] or early 1951, following Chinese involvement in the [Korean War](16772.md).[^cite_note-Kaufman_2001_440–456-82] Operation Paper entailed CIA plans used by CIA military advisors on the ground in Burma to assist Kuomintang incursions into [Western China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_China) over several years, under the command of General [Li Mi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_Mi_\(Republic_of_China_general\)), with Kuomintang leadership hoping to eventually retake China, despite opposition from the US State Department.[^cite_note-Kaufman_2001_440–456-82] However, each attempted invasion was repelled by the Chinese army. The Kuomintang took control of large swaths of Burma, while the government of Burma complained repeatedly of the military invasion to the [United Nations](31769.md).[^cite_note-apjjf.org-83] The CIA hired pilots to secretly transport American weapons and supplies from [Thailand](30128.md) to the [Kuomintang in Burma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuomintang_in_Burma), and [opium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opium) from the Kuomintang to Chinese [organized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triad_\(organized_crime\)) [drug-traffickers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opium_trade) in [Bangkok](56615.md), Thailand, using [CAT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Air_Transport) owned airplanes.[^cite_note-apjjf.org-83][^cite_note-84]

#### 1952: Egypt

Main articles: [Egyptian revolution of 1952](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_revolution_of_1952) and [Project FF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_FF)

In February 1952, following January's [riots in Cairo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairo_fire) amid widespread [nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_nationalism) discontent over the continued [British occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Egypt_under_the_British) of the [Suez Canal](29323.md) and Egypt's defeat in the [1948 Arab–Israeli War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_War), CIA officer [Kermit Roosevelt Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kermit_Roosevelt_Jr.) was dispatched by the [State Department](31975.md) to meet with [Farouk I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farouk_of_Egypt) of the [Kingdom of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Egypt). American policy at that time was to convince Farouk to introduce reforms that would weaken the appeal of Egyptian radicals and stabilize Farouk's grip on power. The U.S. was notified in advance of the successful [July coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_revolution_of_1952) led by nationalist and anti-Communist [Egyptian military officers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Officers_Movement_\(Egypt\)) (the "Free Officers") that replaced the Egyptian monarchy with the [Republic of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Egypt) under the leadership of [Mohamed Naguib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Naguib) and [Gamal Abdel Nasser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gamal_Abdel_Nasser). CIA officer [Miles Copeland Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miles_Copeland_Jr.) recounted in his memoirs that Roosevelt helped coordinate the coup during three prior meetings with the plotters (including Nasser, the future [Egyptian president](335341.md)); this has not been confirmed by declassified documents but is partially supported by circumstantial evidence. Roosevelt and several of the Egyptians said to have been present in these meetings denied Copeland's account; another U.S. official, William Lakeland, said its veracity is open to question. Hugh Wilford notes that "whether or not the CIA dealt directly with the Free Officers *prior* to their July 1952 coup, there was extensive secret American-Egyptian contact in the months *after* the revolution."[^cite_note-85][^cite_note-86]

#### 1952: Guatemala

Main article: [Operation PBFortune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_PBFortune)

[Operation PBFortune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_PBFortune), also known as Operation Fortune, was an aborted [covert United States operation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Covert_United_States_operation) to overthrow [Guatemalan](17238567.md) President [Jacobo Árbenz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacobo_%C3%81rbenz) in 1952. The operation was authorized by [U.S. President](24113.md) [Harry Truman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_Truman) and planned by the [Central Intelligence Agency](5183633.md). The plan involved providing weapons to the exiled Guatemalan military officer [Carlos Castillo Armas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Castillo_Armas), who was to lead an invasion from Nicaragua.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMoulton201347–49-87]

#### 1952–1953: Iran

Main article: [1953 Iranian coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1953_Iranian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat)



Since 1941, [Iran](14653.md) was a constitutional monarchy ruled by the [Shah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah) [Mohammad Reza Pahlavi](20065598.md). From the [discovery of oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalization_of_the_Iranian_oil_industry#Background) in Iran in the late nineteenth century major powers exploited the weakness of the Iranian government to obtain concessions that many believed failed to give Iran a fair share of the profits. During World War II, the UK, the USSR and the US all became involved in Iranian affairs, including the joint [Anglo-Soviet invasion of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Soviet_invasion_of_Iran) in 1941. Iranian officials began to notice that British taxes were increasing while royalties to Iran declined. By 1948, Britain received substantially more revenue from the [Anglo-Iranian Oil Company (AIOC)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Persian_Oil_Company) than Iran. Negotiations to meet this and other Iranian concerns exacerbated rather than eased tensions.[^cite_note-88]

On March 15, 1951, the [Majlis](383276.md), the Iranian parliament, passed legislation championed by reformist politician [Mohammad Mosaddegh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Mosaddegh) to nationalize the AIOC. Fifteen months later, Mosadegh was elected Prime Minister by the Majlis. International business concerns then boycotted oil from the nationalized Iranian oil industry. This contributed to concerns in Britain and the US that Mosadegh might be Communist. He was reportedly supported by the Communist [Tudeh Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tudeh_Party_of_Iran).[^cite_note-newsmine.org-89][^cite_note-90]

The CIA began supporting 18 of their favorite candidates in the [1952 Iranian legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1952_Iranian_legislative_election), which Mosaddegh suspended after urban deputies loyal to him were elected.[^cite_note-91] The new parliament gave Mosaddegh emergency powers which weakened the power of the Shah, and there was a constitutional struggle over the roles of the Shah and prime minister. Britain strongly backed the Shah, while the US officially remained neutral. However, America's position shifted in late 1952 with the election of [Dwight D. Eisenhower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwight_D._Eisenhower) as U.S. president. The CIA launched [Operation Ajax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Ajax), directed by [Kermit Roosevelt Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kermit_Roosevelt_Jr.), with help from [Norman Darbyshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman_Darbyshire), to remove Mosaddegh by persuading the Shah to replace him, using diplomacy and bribery. The [1953 Iranian coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1953_Iranian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat) (known in Iran as the "28 Mordad coup")[^cite_note-92] was instigated by the intelligence agencies of the United Kingdom such as [MI6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MI6) (under the name "Operation Boot") and the United States (under the name "TPAJAX Project").[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-CN-IC-01-94][^cite_note-95][^cite_note-96]

The coup saw the transition of Pahlavi from a [constitutional monarch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_monarchy) to an [authoritarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism), who relied heavily on United States government support. That support dissipated during the [Iranian Revolution](347268.md) of 1979, as his own security forces refused to shoot into non-violent crowds.[^cite_note-97] In August 2013, the CIA admitted its role in the coup "as an act of U.S. foreign policy."[^cite_note-98]

#### 1954: Guatemala

Main article: [1954 Guatemalan coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1954_Guatemalan_coup_d'%C3%A9tat)

See also: [Guatemalan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guatemalan_Civil_War)

In a 1954 CIA operation code named [Operation PBSuccess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_PBSuccess), the U.S. government executed a [coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1954_Guatemalan_coup_d'%C3%A9tat) that successfully overthrew the government of President [Jacobo Árbenz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacobo_%C3%81rbenz), elected in 1950, and installed [Carlos Castillo Armas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Castillo_Armas), the first of a line of right-wing dictators, in its place.[^cite_note-99][^cite_note-100][^cite_note-101] The American government and CIA were motivated by the ideological aim of containment, and by fear of anti-labor exploitation laws reducing profits to the [United Fruit Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Fruit_Company),[^cite_note-talbot-102] which was well connected to the CIA and the Eisenhower administration.[^cite_note-103][^cite_note-talbot-102] In planning the operation, the CIA would become involved in assisting the new regime in the killing of perceived opponents, and lied to the president of the United States when briefing him regarding the number of casualties.[^cite_note-104][^cite_note-CIA-105][^cite_note-106] The perceived success of the operation made it a model for future CIA operations.[^cite_note-CIA-105][^cite_note-talbot-102]

#### 1956–1957: Syria

See also: [CIA activities in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_activities_in_Syria)

In 1956, [Operation Straggle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Straggle) was a failed coup plot against [Nasserist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasserism) civilian politician [Sabri al-Asali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabri_al-Asali). The CIA made plans for a coup for late October 1956 to topple the Syrian government. The plan entailed takeover by the Syrian military of key cities and border crossings.[^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108][^cite_note-Blum,_William_1995_pp._86-87-109] The plan was postponed when [Israel invaded Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Kadesh) in October 1956 and U.S. planners thought their operation would be unsuccessful at a time when the Arab world is fighting "Israeli aggression". The operation was uncovered and American plotters had to flee the country.[^cite_note-110]

In 1957, [Operation Wappen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Wappen) was a second coup plan against Syria, planned by the CIA's [Kermit Roosevelt Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kermit_Roosevelt_Jr.) It called for assassination of key senior Syrian officials, staged military incidents on the Syrian border to be blamed on Syria and then to be used as pretext for invasion by [Iraqi](7515928.md) and [Jordanian](7515964.md) troops, an intense U.S. propaganda campaign targeting the Syrian population, and "sabotage, national conspiracies and various strong-arm activities" to be blamed on Damascus.[^cite_note-Fenton-111][^cite_note-Prados-112][^cite_note-Blum,_William_1995_pp._86-87-109][^cite_note-113] This operation failed when Syrian military officers paid off with millions of dollars in bribes to carry out the coup revealed the plot to Syrian intelligence. The U.S. [Department of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_State) denied accusation of a coup attempt and along with [US media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_of_the_United_States) accused Syria of being a "satellite" of the [USSR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USSR).[^cite_note-Prados-112][^cite_note-114][^cite_note-115]

There was also a third plan in 1957, called "The Preferred Plan". Alongside Britain's [MI6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MI6), the CIA planned to support and arm several uprisings. However, this plan was never carried out.[^cite_note-Fenton-111]

#### 1957–1959: Indonesia

See also: [Permesta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permesta) and [CIA activities in Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_activities_in_Indonesia)



Starting in 1957, Eisenhower ordered the CIA to overthrow [Sukarno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukarno). The CIA supported the failed [Permesta Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permesta_Rebellion) by rebel Indonesian military officers in February 1958. CIA pilots, such as [Allen Lawrence Pope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allen_Lawrence_Pope), piloted planes operated by a CIA [front organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Front_organization), the [Civil Air Transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Air_Transport) (CAT), which bombed civilian and military targets in Indonesia. The CIA instructed CAT pilots to target commercial shipping in order to frighten foreign merchant ships away from Indonesian waters, thereby weakening the [Indonesian economy](14647.md) and thus destabilizing the government of Indonesia. The CIA aerial bombardment resulted in the sinking of several commercial ships[^cite_note-116] and the bombing of a marketplace that killed many civilians.[^cite_note-117] Pope was shot down and captured on 18 May 1958, revealing U.S. involvement, which Eisenhower publicly denied at the time. The rebellion was ultimately defeated by 1961.[^cite_note-118][^cite_note-119]

#### 1959: Iraq

See also: [CIA activities in Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_activities_in_Iraq)

Iraq in its region

Iraq in its region

Concerned about the influence of the [Iraqi Communist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_Communist_Party) (ICP) in Brigadier [Abd al-Karim Qasim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abd_al-Karim_Qasim)'s administration, President Eisenhower questioned that "it might be good policy to help \[[Gamal Abdel Nasser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gamal_Abdel_Nasser)] take over in Iraq," recommending that Nasser be provided with "money and support", thus the U.S. "moved into increasingly close alignment with Egypt with regard to Qasim and Iraq."[^cite_note-WH2021_1959-120] After Iraq withdrew from the anti-Soviet alliance—the [Baghdad Pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baghdad_Pact)—the [United States National Security Council](44026.md) (NSC) proposed various contingencies for preventing a Communist takeover of the country,[^cite_note-121] and "soon developed a detailed plan for assisting nationalist elements committed to the overthrow of Qasim."[^cite_note-WH2021_1959-120] The U.S. also "approached Nasser to discuss 'parallel measures' that could be taken by the two countries against Iraq."[^cite_note-Osgood_pp._21–23-122]

During a NSC meeting on September 24, two representatives from the [State Department](31975.md) urged a cautious approach, while the other twelve representatives, namely from the CIA and the [Department of Defense](7279897.md), "strong\[ly] pitch\[ed] for a more active policy toward Iraq." One CIA representative noted that there is a "small stockpile \[of weapons] in the area," and that the CIA "could support elements in Jordan and the UAR to help Iraqis filter back to Iraq."[^cite_note-Osgood_pp._21–23-122] That same day, the NSC would also prepare a study which called for "covert assistance to Egyptian efforts to topple Qasim," and for "grooming political leadership for a successor government."[^cite_note-WH2021_1959-120] Bryan R. Gibson writes that "there is no documentation that ties the United States directly to any of Nasser's many covert attempts to overthrow the Qasim regime."[^cite_note-123] However, Brandon Wolfe-Hunnicutt states that the U.S. issued its "tacit support for Egyptian efforts to bring \[Qasim's government] down,"[^cite_note-WH2021_1959-120] and Kenneth Osgood writes that "circumstantial evidence in declassified records suggests that ... \[t]he United States was working with Nasser on some level, even if the precise nature of that collaboration is not known."[^cite_note-Osgood_pp._21–23-122] Contemporary documents pertaining to the CIA's operations in Iraq have remained classified or heavily redacted, thus "allow\[ing] for plausible deniability."[^cite_note-Osgood_p._16-124]

[Richard Sale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Sale_\(journalist\)) of [United Press International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Press_International) (UPI), citing former U.S. diplomat and intelligence officials, [Adel Darwish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adel_Darwish), and other experts, reported that the unsuccessful October 7, 1959 assassination attempt on Qasim involving a young [Saddam Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein) and other Ba'athist conspirators was a collaboration between the CIA and [Egyptian intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Intelligence_Directorate_\(Egypt\)).[^cite_note-125] Gibson has disputed Sale and Darwish's account, concluding that available declassified records show that "while the United States was aware of several plots against Qasim, it had still adhered to \[a] nonintervention policy."[^cite_note-126] Wolfe-Hunnicutt observes that "\[i]t seems more likely that it was October 7 that brought the Ba'ath to the attention of the US government."[^cite_note-127] On the other hand, Osgood writes that "the circumstantial evidence is such that the possibility of US–UAR collaboration with Ba'ath Party activists cannot be ruled out," concluding that: "Whatever the validity of \[Sale's] charges, at the very least currently declassified documents reveal that US officials were actively considering various plots against Qasim and that the CIA was building up assets for covert operations in Iraq."[^cite_note-Osgood_pp._21–23-122]

The assassins, including Saddam, escaped to [Cairo](6293.md), Egypt "where they enjoyed Nasser's protection for the remainder of Qasim's tenure in power."[^cite_note-WH2021_1959_2-128] One of the conspirators involved in the assassination attempt, Hazim Jawad, "received training from the UAR intelligence service in clandestine wireless telegraphy," before returning to Iraq in 1960 to coordinate "clandestine radio operations for the UAR." Wolfe-Hunnicutt writes that in the 1959–1960 period, during the "peak of US-UAR intelligence collaboration ... \[i]t is quite possible that Jawad became familiar to US intelligence," as a 1963 State Department cable described him as "one of our boys."[^cite_note-129] Similarly, it is possible that Saddam visited the U.S. embassy in Cairo,[^cite_note-130] and some evidence suggests that he was "in frequent contact with US officials and intelligence agents."[^cite_note-Osgood_pp._21–23-122] A former high-ranking U.S. official told Marion Farouk–Sluglett and Peter Sluglett that Iraqi Ba'athists, including Saddam, "had made contact with the American authorities in the late 1950s and early 1960s."[^cite_note-Slugletts_p._327-131]

#### 1959–1963: South Vietnam

Main articles: [War in Vietnam (1959–1963)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Vietnam_\(1959%E2%80%931963\)), [Buddhist crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist_crisis), and [1963 South Vietnamese coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1963_South_Vietnamese_coup_d'%C3%A9tat)

See also: [1962 South Vietnamese Independence Palace bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1962_South_Vietnamese_Independence_Palace_bombing), [January 1964 South Vietnamese coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_1964_South_Vietnamese_coup), [December 1964 South Vietnamese coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/December_1964_South_Vietnamese_coup), and [1965 South Vietnamese coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1965_South_Vietnamese_coup)

In 1959 a branch of the [Worker's Party of Vietnam](400546.md) was formed in the south of the country and began an insurgency against the [Republic of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Vietnam) (South Vietnam).[^cite_note-HistPlace-132] They were supplied through [Group 559](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_559), which was formed the same year by North Vietnam to send weapons down the [Ho Chi Minh trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ho_Chi_Minh_trail).[^cite_note-Prados2-133][^cite_note-HRW-RoM-Ch03-134] The US supported the RoV against the communists. After the 1960 US election, President [John F. Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_F._Kennedy) became much more involved with the fight against the insurgency.[^cite_note-Shultz-135]



Location of South Vietnam

From mid-1963, the Kennedy administration became increasingly frustrated with South Vietnamese President [Ngo Dinh Diem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ngo_Dinh_Diem)'s repressive rule and his [perceived persecution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist_crisis) of the [Buddhists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_Vietnam). In light of Diem's refusal to adopt reforms, American officials debated whether they should support efforts to replace him. These debates crystallized after the [ARVN Special Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ARVN_Special_Forces), which took their orders directly from the palace, [raided Buddhist temples across the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xa_Loi_Pagoda_raids), leaving a death toll estimated in the hundreds, and resulted in the dispatch of [Cable 243](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cable_243) on August 24, 1963, which instructed [United States Ambassador to South Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Ambassador_to_South_Vietnam), [Henry Cabot Lodge Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Cabot_Lodge_Jr.), to "examine all possible alternative leadership and make detailed plans as to how we might bring about Diem's replacement if this should become necessary". Lodge and his liaison officer, [Lucien Conein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucien_Conein), contacted discontented [Army of the Republic of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_of_the_Republic_of_Vietnam) officers and gave assurances that the US would not oppose a coup or respond with aid cuts. These efforts culminated in [a coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1963_South_Vietnamese_coup) on November 1–2, 1963, during which Diem and [his brother](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ng%C3%B4_%C4%90%C3%ACnh_Nhu) were [assassinated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrest_and_assassination_of_Ngo_Dinh_Diem).[^cite_note-136] By the end of 1963 the Viet Cong switched to a much more aggressive strategy in fighting the Southern government and the US.

The [Pentagon Papers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentagon_Papers) concluded that "Beginning in August of 1963 we variously authorized, sanctioned and encouraged the coup efforts of the Vietnamese generals and offered full support for a successor government. In October we cut off aid to Diem in a direct rebuff, giving a green light to the generals. We maintained clandestine contact with them throughout the planning and execution of the coup and sought to review their operational plans and proposed new government."[^cite_note-137]

#### 1959–1962: Cuba

Main articles: [Cuban Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuban_Project), [Bay of Pigs Invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_of_Pigs_Invasion), and [Assassination attempts on Fidel Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_attempts_on_Fidel_Castro)



Location of Bay of Pigs in Cuba

[Fulgencio Batista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fulgencio_Batista) was a [military dictator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_dictator) who seized power in [Cuba](5042481.md) in March 1952 via a [coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1952_Cuban_coup_d'%C3%A9tat) and was backed by the U.S. government until March 1958. His regime was overthrown on December 31, 1958, thus bringing an end to the [Cuban Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuban_Revolution) that was led by [Fidel Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidel_Castro) and his [26th of July Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/26th_of_July_Movement). Castro became president in February 1959. The CIA backed a force composed of CIA-trained [Cuban exiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuban_exile) to [invade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_of_Pigs_Invasion) Cuba with support and equipment from the US military, in an attempt to overthrow [Castro's government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuba_under_Fidel_Castro). The invasion was launched in April 1961, three months after [John F. Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_F._Kennedy) assumed the presidency in the United States, but the [Cuban armed forces](73251.md) defeated the invading combatants within three days.[^cite_note-138]

[Operation MONGOOSE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_MONGOOSE) was a year-long U.S. government effort to overthrow the government of Cuba.[^cite_note-139] The operation included an [embargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embargo) against Cuba, "to induce failure of the [Communist regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_regime) to supply Cuba's economic needs", a diplomatic initiative to isolate Cuba, and [psychological operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychological_operations) "to turn the peoples' resentment increasingly against the regime."[^cite_note-history.state.gov2-140] The economic warfare prong of the operation also included the infiltration of CIA operatives to carry out many acts of sabotage against civilian targets, such as a [railway bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Railway_bridge), a [molasses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molasses) storage facilities, an [electric power plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_power_plant), and the [sugar](27712.md) harvest, notwithstanding Cuba's repeated requests to the United States government to cease its armed operations.[^cite_note-141][^cite_note-history.state.gov2-140] In addition, the CIA planned a number of [assassination attempts against Fidel Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_attempts_against_Fidel_Castro), head of government of Cuba, including attempts that entailed CIA collaboration with the [American mafia](18293303.md).[^cite_note-142][^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144] In April 2021, documents released by the [National Security Archive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Archive) showed that the CIA was also involved in a plot to assassinate [Raúl Castro](221749.md) in 1960.[^cite_note-145]

#### 1959: Cambodia

Main article: [Bangkok Plot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangkok_Plot)



Ngô Đình Nhu meeting US vice president Lyndon Johnson in 1961

In December 1958, [Ngô Đình Nhu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ng%C3%B4_%C4%90%C3%ACnh_Nhu), who was the younger brother and chief adviser of [Ngo Dinh Diem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ngo_Dinh_Diem) (the president of South Vietnam), broached the idea of a coup ([Bangkok Plot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangkok_Plot)) to overthrow Cambodian leader [Norodom Sihanouk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norodom_Sihanouk).[^cite_note-146] Nhu contacted [Dap Chhuon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dap_Chhuon), Sihanouk's [Interior Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Interior_\(Cambodia\)), who was known for his pro-American sympathies, to prepare for the coup against his boss.[^cite_note-147] Chhuon received covert financial and military assistance from [Thailand](30128.md), [South Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Vietnam), and the CIA.[^cite_note-148] In January 1959 Sihanouk learned of the coup plans through intermediaries who were in contact with Chhuon.[^cite_note-149] The following month, Sihanouk sent the army to capture Chhuon, who was summarily executed as soon as he was captured, effectively ending the coup attempt.[^cite_note-150] Sihanouk then accused South Vietnam and the U.S. of planning the coup attempt.[^cite_note-151] Six months later, on 31 August 1959, a small packaged lacquer gift, which was fitted with a [parcel bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parcel_bomb), was delivered to the royal palace. An investigation traced the origin of the parcel bomb to an American military base in [Saigon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saigon).[^cite_note-152] While Sihanouk publicly accused Ngo Dinh Nhu of masterminding the bomb attack, he secretly suspected that the U.S. was also involved.[^cite_note-153] The incident deepened his distrust of the U.S.[^cite_note-154]

### 1960s

#### 1960–1965: Congo-Leopoldville

Main articles: [Patrice Lumumba](49240.md) and [Congo Crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congo_Crisis)



[Patrice Lumumba](49240.md) was elected the first Prime Minister of the [Republic of the Congo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_the_Congo_\(L%C3%A9opoldville\)), now the [Democratic Republic of the Congo](76762.md), in May 1960, and in June 1960, the country achieved full independence from [Belgium](3343.md).[^cite_note-Reid2023-155]<sup>: 4, 104–6 </sup> In July, the [Congo Crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congo_Crisis) erupted with a mutiny among army, followed by the regions [Katanga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Katanga) and [South Kasai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Kasai) seceding with support from Belgium, who wished to keep power over resources in the region.[^cite_note-Reid2023-155]<sup>: 127, 150–1, 278 </sup> Lumumba called in the [United Nations](31769.md) to help him, but the U.N. force only agreed to keep peace and not stop the separatist movements. Lumumba then agreed to receive help from the USSR in order to stop the separatists, worrying the United States, due to the supply of [uranium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uranium_ore) in the country. The CIA sent official [Sydney Gottlieb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Gottlieb) with a poison to liaison with an African CIA [asset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asset_\(intelligence\)) code-named WI/Rogue who was to assassinate Lumumba, but Lumumba went into hiding before the operation was completed.[^cite_note-156]

After Lumumba was killed, the US began funding [Mobutu Sese Seko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobutu_Sese_Seko) in order to secure him against the separatists and opposition.
