Since 1996, [Afghanistan](737.md) had been under the control of the [Taliban](30635.md)-led [Islamic Emirate of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Emirate_of_Afghanistan_\(1996%E2%80%932001\)), a largely [unrecognized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_with_limited_recognition) [unitary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state) [Deobandi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deobandi)–[Islamic](2646438.md) [theocratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theocracy) [emirate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emirate) administered by *[shura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shura)* councils.[^cite_note-Afghanistan_After_the_Western_Drawd-420] On October 7, 2001, four weeks after the [September 11 attacks](5058690.md) by [al-Qaeda](1921.md), [the United States invaded Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_invasion_of_Afghanistan) and began bombing al-Qaeda and Taliban targets. Under the Taliban regime, al-Qaeda had used Afghanistan to train and indoctrinate fighters at its own training camps, import weapons, coordinate with other [jihadists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jihadist), and plot terrorist actions. 10,000 to 20,000 men passed through al-Qaeda run camps before 9/11, most of whom went to fight for the Taliban, while a smaller number were inducted into al-Qaeda.[^cite_note-421] Although none of the hijackers were of Afghan nationality, the attacks had been planned in [Kandahar](17260.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEColl2004473–478,_490-422] [George W. Bush](3414021.md) said that the goal was to capture al-Qaeda leader [Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden) and bring him to justice.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEColl2004583–584-423]

On October 11, four days after the bombing started, Bush claimed that it might stop if bin Laden were handed over to the U.S. by the Taliban, which had provided safe haven to al-Qaeda. "If you cough him up and his people today, then we'll reconsider what we are doing to your country," Bush told the Taliban. "You still have a second chance. Just bring him in, and bring his leaders and lieutenants and other thugs and criminals with him."[^cite_note-424] On October 14, Bush turned down an offer from the Taliban to discuss sending bin Laden to a third country.[^cite_note-425] Taliban leader [Mullah Omar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mullah_Omar) had previously refused to extradite bin Laden.[^cite_note-426] The United Kingdom was a key ally of the United States, offering support for military action from the start of preparations for the invasion, and the two countries worked with anti-Taliban Afghan forces in the [Northern Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Alliance).[^cite_note-auto2-427] The US aimed to destroy al-Qaeda and remove the Taliban regime from power,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright_et_al.201041-428] but also sought to prevent the Northern Alliance from taking control of Afghanistan, believing the Alliance's rule would alienate the country's Pashtun majority.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodward2002122-429] CIA director [George Tenet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Tenet) argued that the US should target al-Qaeda but "hold off on the Taliban," since the Taliban were popular in Pakistan and attacking them could jeopardize [relations with Pakistan](7144235.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodward2002123-430] By the end of October, a further goal had emerged: to remove the Taliban from power in Afghanistan.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright_et_al.201041-428]

From December 6–17, 2001, a team of Northern Alliance fighters, under direction from a U.S. special forces team, pursued bin Laden in the [cave complex of Tora Bora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Tora_Bora) in eastern Afghanistan, but the U.S. did not commit its own troops to the operation and bin Laden escaped to neighbouring [Pakistan](23235.md).[^cite_note-431] That same month, the Taliban Islamic Emirate of Afghanistan fell[^cite_note-auto2-427] and was replaced by the [Afghan Interim Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_Interim_Administration) and then the [Transitional Islamic State of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transitional_Islamic_State_of_Afghanistan) in 2002, and finally the [Islamic Republic of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Republic_of_Afghanistan) in 2004. Bin Laden was killed by a team of [United States Navy SEALs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Navy_SEALs) in a raid on his clandestine residence in [Abbottabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbottabad), Pakistan, in May 2011, nearly ten years after the initial invasion.[^cite_note-auto2-427] Despite bin Laden's death, the U.S. remained in Afghanistan, propping up the governments of [Hamid Karzai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamid_Karzai) and [Ashraf Ghani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashraf_Ghani).[^cite_note-432] President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) struck an arrangement with the Taliban in February 2020 that would see U.S. troops [withdraw from Afghanistan](66883576.md).[^cite_note-433] In April 2021, his successor, [Joe Biden](145422.md) announced that a full withdrawal would occur in August of that year.[^cite_note-434] This was followed by the return of the Taliban to power.[^cite_note-auto2-427]

#### 2002: Venezuela

This section is an excerpt from [2002 Venezuelan coup attempt § US role and alleged involvement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_Venezuelan_coup_attempt#US_role_and_alleged_involvement).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2002_Venezuelan_coup_attempt\&action=edit)]

President Chávez asserted numerous times that United States government officials knew about plans for a coup, approved of them, and assumed they would be successful,[^cite_note-2002_Venezuelan_coup_attempt_Ref_ao-435] alleging that "two military officers from the United States" were present in the headquarters of coup plotters.[^cite_note-2002_Venezuelan_coup_attempt_Ref_ap-436] Rear Admiral Carlos Molina, a central leader of the coup, later said: "We felt we were acting with US support ... we agree that we can't permit a communist government here. The US has not let us down yet."[^cite_note-2002_Venezuelan_coup_attempt_Ref_aq-437] The United States repeatedly informed the Venezuelan opposition that they would not be supported if there were a coup,[^cite_note-2002_Venezuelan_coup_attempt_DocumentsShow-438][^cite_note-2002_Venezuelan_coup_attempt_Al_Jazeera-439] and following the coup attempt President Bush denied any US involvement.[^cite_note-2002_Venezuelan_coup_attempt_aljazeera20090921-440]

On 27 April 2002, chairman [Cass Ballenger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cass_Ballenger) and Congressman [Bill Delahunt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Delahunt) of the United States met with Venezuelan media heads of [Venevisión](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venevisi%C3%B3n), [Globovisión](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globovisi%C3%B3n), Unión Radio, *[El Nacional](4277061.md)*, *[Últimas Noticias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%9Altimas_Noticias)*, and *[El Mundo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Mundo_\(Spain\))*, telling them that "the U.S. was opposed to any disruption of constitutional government and would condemn any coup, open or disguised, aimed at ousting Chávez".[^cite_note-2002_Venezuelan_coup_attempt_USdosDINNER-441] At a meeting soon after the coup between Ambassador Shapiro and then Venezuelan Vice President [José Vicente Rangel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Vicente_Rangel) at the Vice President's home, Rangel also stated to Shapiro that "no one in the upper echelons of the Venezuelan government really believed that the United States was involved in the attempted overthrow" and that if the Venezuelan government did believe so, "the two men wouldn't have been sitting in Rangel's house".[^cite_note-2002_Venezuelan_coup_attempt_FPcoupFATIGUE-442] Unlike much of Latin America, the US refused to condemn the coup, changing its position only after Carmona resigned.[^cite_note-443]

Unnamed [Organisation of American States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_of_American_States) officials and other diplomatic sources told *[The Observer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Observer)* that the coup was "tied to senior officials in the US government" and that the US was not only aware of the coup, but also gave sanction to its organizers. The paper named [Elliot Abrams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliot_Abrams), who had been convicted of deceiving Congress during the [Iran Contra Affair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Contra_Affair), as having greenlit the coup.[^cite_note-2002_Venezuelan_coup_attempt_Venezuela_coup_linked_to_Bush_team-444] Bush administration officials acknowledged meeting with some opposition leaders in the several weeks prior to 11 April but have strongly denied encouraging the coup itself, saying that they insisted on constitutional means.[^cite_note-445] The purpose of the meetings was not clarified, and it is not known why US officials and the Venezuelan opposition broached the subject of a coup months before the attempted ousting took place.[^cite_note-446] *The New York Times* quotes an anonymous Defense Department official in charge of developing policy towards Venezuela as saying: "We were not discouraging people. ... We were sending informal, subtle signals that we don't like this guy. We didn't say, 'No, don't you dare'", though he denied the Defense Department offered material help, such as weaponry.[^cite_note-447]

An investigation conducted by the US [inspector general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inspector_general), requested by US Senator [Christopher Dodd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Dodd), reviewed American activities leading up to and during the coup attempt. The OIG report found no "wrongdoing" by US officials either in the State Department or in the embassy, and concluded: "While it is clear that [NED](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Endowment_for_Democracy)'s, [DOD](7279897.md)'s, and other U.S. assistance programs provided training, institution building, and other support to organizations and individuals understood to be actively involved in the events of April 11–14, we found no evidence that this support directly contributed, or was intended to contribute, to those events. NED is, however, mindful of the fact that, in some circumstances, its efforts to assist specific organizations, or foster open elections, could be perceived as partisan."[^cite_note-448]

#### 2003–2021: Iraq

Main article: [Iraq War](5043324.md)

In 1998 as a non-covert measure, the U.S. enacted the "[Iraq Liberation Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Liberation_Act)", which states, in part, that "It should be the policy of the United States to support efforts to remove the regime headed by Saddam Hussein from power in Iraq," and appropriated funds for U.S. aid "to the Iraqi democratic opposition organizations."[^cite_note-449] After Bush was elected he started being more aggressive toward Iraq.[^cite_note-450] After the 9/11 attacks the Bush administration claimed that Iraq's ruler at the time, [Saddam Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein), had connections to [Al-Qaeda](1921.md) and was supporting terrorism. The administration also stated that Hussein was covertly continuing production of [weapons of mass destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weapon_of_mass_destruction) despite the fact that evidence for both was not conclusive.[^cite_note-Woodward2004Chap1-451][^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453][^cite_note-blix1-454][^cite_note-455] Iraq was also one of the three countries Bush called out in his [Axis of Evil Speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axis_of_evil).[^cite_note-456] In 2002 Congress passed the "[Iraq Resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authorization_for_Use_of_Military_Force_Against_Iraq_Resolution_of_2002)" which authorized the president to "use any means necessary" against Iraq. The Iraq War then began in March 2003 when a United States-led military coalition [invaded the country](201936.md) and overthrew the Iraqi government.[^cite_note-oil-457] The U.S. captured and helped prosecute Hussein, who was later hanged. The U.S. and the new Iraqi government also fought an insurgency following the invasion. In December 2011 the U.S. withdrew its soldiers from the conflict,[^cite_note-458] but returned in 2014 to help stop the rise of the [Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant](9087364.md) (ISIL).[^cite_note-459] The military's combat mission came to an end on December 9, 2021.[^cite_note-460]

#### 2003: Somalia

With the start of the [Somali Civil War](3070332.md), a movement known as the [Islamic Courts Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Courts_Union) emerged in [Mogadishu](60154.md) with the aim of asserting a stable governance against the rule of Somali warlords and the anarchy that came with the overthrow of [Siad Barre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siad_Barre). The Islamic Courts Union's governance was based on providing stability through the implementation of [Sharia](28840.md). Despite the Islamic Courts Union often being called jihadist, the ICU did not implement very rigid interpretations of Sharia or prohibit women's employment, with Ted Dagne, an Africa Research Specialist for the Congressional Research Service, remarking that "the leadership \[of the ICU] was often referred to as jihadist, extremist, and at times terrorist by some observers without much evidence to support the allegations. For example, the assessment of the Islamic Courts by U.S. officials was that less than 5% of the Islamic Courts leadership can be considered extremist, according to a senior State Department official."[^cite_note-\:20-461]

Despite this, the ascendance of an [Islamist](15012.md) political force in Somalia during this period was perceived as a threat to Western strategic interests in the [Horn of Africa](62142.md).[^cite_note-462] Within the framework of the [war on terror](13425800.md), the U.S. government also perceived the rise of an Islamic movement in Somalia as a potential terror risk. From 2003 onwards, the [Central Intelligence Agency](5183633.md) (CIA) initiated covert operations against the Islamic Courts Union, aiming to depose them from power.[^cite_note-\:22-463] The [Bush administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_George_W._Bush) had become increasingly concerned with the growing power of the Islamic Courts Union, and feared that they would make Somalia a haven for [Al-Qaeda](1921.md) to plan attacks from, like in [Afghanistan](737.md).[^cite_note-\:2-189][^cite_note-\:3-464] American support for the warlords extended to the point where, on numerous occasions, [Nairobi](21482.md)-based CIA officers landed on warlord-controlled airstrips in Mogadishu with large amounts of money for distribution to Somali militias.[^cite_note-\:5-465] According to [John Prendergast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Prendergast_\(activist\)), CIA-operated flights into Somalia had been bringing in \$100,000 to US\$150,000 per month for the warlords and he further claimed that the flights remained in Somalia for the day so that CIA agents can confer with them.[^cite_note-\:3-464] Many of the warlords the Americans funded to fight the Islamic Courts Union had fought directly against the Americans in Mogadishu during [UNOSOM II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Operation_in_Somalia_II) in 1993.[^cite_note-\:2-189]

American efforts began with a wave of assassinations, resulting in claims by the ICU that America was funding warlords to depose their rule. According to C. Barnes & H. Hassan, "It was in this context that a military force known as [Al-Shabaab](8726463.md) (‘the Youth’) emerged, related to but seemingly autonomous of the broad based Courts movement." With CIA support, the warlords in 2006 formed the [Alliance for the Restoration of Peace and Counter-Terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_for_the_Restoration_of_Peace_and_Counter-Terrorism) and attempted to overthrow the ICU with American backing. However, American efforts to overthrow the Islamic Courts Union in 2006 failed, as the ICU defeated the warlords America supported against them in the [Second Battle of Mogadishu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Battle_of_Mogadishu). American efforts to counter the ICU would not end here, however, as America would continue to support the overthrow of the ICU by supporting an Ethiopian invasion of the country. Leaked documents in 2006 revealed a confidential meeting between senior American and Ethiopian officials in [Addis Ababa](52593.md) six months prior to the full scale December 2006 invasion. Participants deliberated on various scenarios, with the "worst-case scenario" being the potential takeover of Somalia by the Islamic Courts Union. The documents revealed that the US found the prospect unacceptable and would back Ethiopia in the event of an ICU takeover. Journalist [Jon Snow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Snow_\(journalist\)) reported that during the meeting "the blueprint for a very American supported Ethiopian invasion of Somalia was hatched".[^cite_note-Khayre-2014-466]

Pentagon officials and intelligence analysts reported that the invasion had been planned during the summer of 2006 and that [US special forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Army_Special_Forces) were on the ground before the Ethiopians had invaded.[^cite_note-The_Guardian-467] America participated in the invasion of Somalia, as the [Bush Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_George_W._Bush) covertly deployed US Special Forces backed by [AC-130](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_AC-130) gunships in support of [ENDF](9405.md) forces. The lightly armed court forces were unable to counter ENDF/US [air supremacy](537478.md) and armor. The timing and scale of the attack surprised many international observers, leading many to conclude that it was 'fairly obvious that Ethiopia had received significant help' during the invasion.[^cite_note-\:27-468] [Reuters](18998750.md) reported American and British Special Forces, along with US-hired [mercenaries](73374.md), had been laying the ground work for the invasion within and outside Somalia since late 2005. During the invasion the United States provided satellite surveillance of ICU forces to the ENDF, along with extensive military and [logistical support](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_logistics) extending to the provision of spare parts. The European Union was reportedly 'exceptionally unhappy' about the heavy US support for the invasion, and held back funds for the newly created [AMISOM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Union_Mission_to_Somalia) mission for several months.[^cite_note-\:26-469] The US-backed Ethiopian invasion was eventually defeated by Somali insurgents; however, the ICU was overthrown and was unable to re-assert its control over Somalia and Mogadishu by the time of the Ethiopian withdrawal.

#### 2004: Haiti

Main articles: [2004 Haitian coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Haitian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat) and [Operation Secure Tomorrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Secure_Tomorrow)

In February 2004, the democratically elected President of Haiti [Jean-Bertrand Aristide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Bertrand_Aristide) was forced to resign amid a rapidly spiraling situation with the rebel group [National Revolutionary Front for the Liberation and Reconstruction of Haiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Revolutionary_Front_for_the_Liberation_and_Reconstruction_of_Haiti), which had laid siege to the capital [Port-au-Prince](57391.md) in the week leading up to Aristide's resignation.[^cite_note-470] It was alleged that the rebels were trained by [U.S. Special Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_special_operations_forces) in the nearby [Dominican Republic](8060.md) prior to the instabilities.[^cite_note-471] It has also been alleged by multiple Haitian and French officials,[^cite_note-472] as well as Aristide himself,[^cite_note-473] that the coup was effectively orchestrated by France and the United States. In the aftermath of the coup, a Multinational Interim Force led by the U.S. [invaded Haiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Secure_Tomorrow) to stabilize the country under the government of the newly assumed provisional president [Boniface Alexandre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boniface_Alexandre) and prime minister [Gérard Latortue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%A9rard_Latortue).

#### 2005: Kyrgyzstan

Main article: [Tulip Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulip_Revolution)

In [Kyrgyzstan](170131.md), in response to the corruption and authoritarianism of the [Askar Akayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Askar_Akayev) government, which had ruled since 1990, mass protests ousted the government and [free elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Kyrgyz_presidential_election) were held. According to *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)*, the US government provided aid to opposition protesters via the [State Department](31975.md), [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USAID), [Radio Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Free_Europe/Radio_Liberty), and [Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House) by funding the only print-media outlet in the country not controlled by the government. When the state cut off electricity to the outlet, the U.S. embassy provided emergency generators. Other opposition groups and an opposition TV station received funding from the US government and US-based NGOs.[^cite_note-474]

#### 2006–2007: Palestine

Main article: [Fatah–Hamas conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatah%E2%80%93Hamas_conflict)



Occupied Palestinian territories

The Bush Administration was displeased with the government formed by Hamas, which won 56% of the seats in the [Palestinian legislative election of 2006](38945340.md).[^cite_note-475] The U.S. government pressured the [Fatah](11104.md) faction of the [Palestinian National Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_National_Authority) leadership to topple the [Hamas](13913.md) government of Prime Minister [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md), and provided funding,[^cite_note-csmonitor.com-476][^cite_note-477] including a secret training and armaments program that received tens of millions of dollars in [congressional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Congress) funding. This funding was initially blocked by Congress, who feared that arms provided to Palestinians might later be used against Israel, but the Bush administration circumvented Congress.[^cite_note-vanityfair.com-478][^cite_note-479][^cite_note-480]

Fatah launched a war against the Haniyeh government. When the government of [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) attempted to negotiate a truce between the sides so as to avoid a wide-scale Palestinian civil war, the U.S. government pressured Fatah to reject the Saudi plan and to continue the effort to topple the Hamas government.[^cite_note-vanityfair.com-478] Ultimately, the [Hamas government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas_government_of_June_2007) was prevented from ruling over all of the [Palestinian territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_territories), with Fatah retreating to the [West Bank](33209.md) and Hamas retreating to and taking control of the [Gaza Strip](12047.md).[^cite_note-481]

#### 2005–2009: Syria

In 2005, after a period of co-operation in the [war on terror](13425800.md), the Bush administration froze relations with Syria. According to US cables released by [WikiLeaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WikiLeaks), the State Department then began to funnel money to opposition groups, including at least \$6 million to the opposition satellite channel [Barada TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barada_TV) and the exile group [Movement for Justice and Development in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movement_for_Justice_and_Development_in_Syria), although this was denied by the channel.[^cite_note-WaPoWikiLeaks-482][^cite_note-483][^cite_note-484] This alleged covert backing continued under the Obama administration until at least April 2009 when US diplomats expressed concern the funding would undermine US attempts to rebuild relations with Syrian President [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md).[^cite_note-WaPoWikiLeaks-482]

#### 2009: Honduras

In 2017, *[The Intercept](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Intercept)* reported that military officials from the [Center for Hemispheric Defense Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_J._Perry_Center_for_Hemispheric_Defense_Studies) had quietly assisted Honduran officers in a [coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Honduran_coup_d'%C3%A9tat) against President [Manuel Zelaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Zelaya) on 28 June 2009, helping them argue the coup's legality and arrange meetings with sympathetic U.S. lawmakers.[^cite_note-485]

### 2010s

#### 2010: Australia

Main article: [2010 Australian Labor Party leadership spill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Australian_Labor_Party_leadership_spill)

Multiple leftist publications like *[Jacobin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacobin_\(magazine\)), [World Socialist Web Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Socialist_Web_Site)* and [Jordan Shanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friendlyjordies) have alleged that the ousting of [Australian prime minister](24117.md) [Kevin Rudd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Rudd) in the [2010 Labor leadership challenge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Australian_Labor_Party_leadership_spill) was influenced by US involvement.[^cite_note-486][^cite_note-487]

Later that year [WikiLeaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WikiLeaks) [leaked multiple classified cables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_diplomatic_cables_leak) from the [US State Department](31975.md) which revealed that several key members of the [Labor Right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_Right) that were responsible for the removal of Rudd and installation of [deputy prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deputy_Prime_Minister_of_Australia) and Labor Right member [Julia Gillard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_Gillard) as Labor leader and new prime minister were "protected sources" and in regular exchange with the [US embassy in Canberra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_the_United_States,_Canberra).[^cite_note-488] [Mark Arbib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Arbib), who has been considered kingmaker in the "Gillard coup", has reportedly been secretly briefing the US embassy about the state of [Rudd's government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudd_government_\(2007%E2%80%932010\)) and possible leadership challenges to Rudd.[^cite_note-489][^cite_note-490]

#### 2011: Libya

Main articles: [2011 military intervention in Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_military_intervention_in_Libya) and [American involvement in the 2011 Libyan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_involvement_in_the_2011_Libyan_Civil_War)



In 2011, Libya had been led by [Muammar Gaddafi](53029.md) since 1969. In February 2011, amid the "[Arab Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Spring)", a revolution broke out against him, spreading from the second city [Benghazi](204114.md) (where an [interim government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Transitional_Council) was set up on February 27), to the capital [Tripoli](31232.md), sparking the [First Libyan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Libyan_Civil_War). On March 17, [United Nations Security Council Resolution 1973](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1973) was adopted, authorizing a [no-fly zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No-fly_zone) over Libya, and "all necessary measures" to protect civilians.[^cite_note-UN-AllNecessaryMeasures-491] Two days later, France, the United States and the United Kingdom launched the [2011 military intervention in Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_military_intervention_in_Libya) with [Operation Odyssey Dawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Odyssey_Dawn), US and British naval forces firing over 110 [Tomahawk cruise missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomahawk_\(missile_family\)),[^cite_note-al_jaz_command-492] the French and British Air Forces[^cite_note-493] undertaking [sorties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorties) across Libya and a naval [blockade](293054.md) by Coalition forces.[^cite_note-cnn_deployment-494] A coalition of 27 states from Europe and the Middle East soon joined the [NATO](21133.md)-led intervention, as [Operation Unified Protector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Unified_Protector). The Gaddafi government collapsed in August, leaving the National Transitional Council as the de facto government, with UN recognition. Gaddafi was captured and [killed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Muammar_Gaddafi) in October by National Transitional Council forces and NATO action ceased.[^cite_note-unscres1973-495][^cite_note-496]

#### 2011–2017: Syria

Main articles: [Timber Sycamore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timber_Sycamore), [CIA activities in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_activities_in_Syria), and [American-led intervention in the Syrian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American-led_intervention_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War)



In April 2011, after the outbreak of the [Syrian civil war](30741795.md) in early 2011, three U.S. Senators, Republicans [John McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McCain) and [Lindsey Graham](363279.md) and Independent [Joe Lieberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Lieberman), urged President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) in a joint statement to "state unequivocally" that "it is time to go" for President [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md).[^cite_note-497][^cite_note-498] In August 2011, the U.S. government called on Assad to "step aside" and imposed an [oil embargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_embargo) against the Syrian government.[^cite_note-499][^cite_note-500][^cite_note-501] Starting in 2013, the U.S. provided training, weapons, and money to vetted moderate Syrian rebels,[^cite_note-502][^cite_note-503] and in 2014, the [Supreme Military Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Military_Council_\(Syria\)).[^cite_note-504][^cite_note-505] In 2015, Obama reaffirmed that "Assad must go".[^cite_note-506]

In March 2017, Ambassador [Nikki Haley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikki_Haley) told a group of reporters that the US's priority in Syria was no longer on "getting Assad out."[^cite_note-507] Earlier that day at a news conference in [Ankara](802.md), Secretary of State [Rex Tillerson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_Tillerson) also said that the "longer term status of President Assad will be decided by the Syrian people."[^cite_note-508] While the [US Defense Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Defense_Department)'s program to aid predominantly [Kurdish](17068.md) rebels fighting the [Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant](9087364.md) (ISIL) continued, it was revealed in July 2017 that US President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) had ordered a "phasing out" of the CIA's support for anti-Assad rebels.[^cite_note-509] Ultimately, the Assad regime fell in the [2024 Syrian opposition offensives](78460726.md) spearheaded by [Hay'at Tahrir al-Sham](53015755.md) (HTS), the [Syrian National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_National_Army) (SNA), and the [Southern Operations Room](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Operations_Room) (SOR).

#### 2019: Bolivia

Main article: [2019 Bolivian political crisis](62307909.md)

In November 2019, Bolivian president [Evo Morales](284163.md) resigned in what he and some US lawmakers called a coup; Morales then went into exile in Argentina.[^cite_note-510] Morales claimed the following month that the U.S. had plotted to oust him for choosing to seek lithium extraction partnerships with Russia and China.[^cite_note-511] In the aftermath, following the elections which brought back the socialist [Movimiento al Socialismo](2024693.md) to power, the coup narrative was proven false, but the U.S. still faced allegations that it had supported Morales' removal, including Justice Department pressure against MIT researchers whose work argued that Morales' reelection for a fourth term was legitimate.[^cite_note-512][^cite_note-513]

### 2020s

#### 2025–2026: Greenland

Main article: [Greenland crisis](82044223.md)

See also: [Proposed United States acquisition of Greenland](61530125.md)

As president in 2025, Trump renewed annexation threats against Greenland and refused to rule out military force. After the military intervention removing Maduro from office in 2026, Trump again threatened military action "whether they like it or not".[^cite_note-514] The United States in 2025 had also set up influencers and intelligence agencies to spy in Greenland and perform "covert influence operations", resulting in two US ambassador summons by Denmark.[^cite_note-515][^cite_note-516]

#### 2025–2026: Venezuela

Main articles: [Operation Southern Spear](81593523.md) and [2026 United States intervention in Venezuela](82021123.md)

See also: [Proposed United States invasion of Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposed_United_States_invasion_of_Venezuela)



During the [2025 United States naval deployment in the Caribbean](80987786.md), Trump stated in October that he had authorized the CIA to conduct covert operations in Venezuela, which Maduro denounced.[^cite_note-517][^cite_note-518] On 3 January 2026, the United States conducted airstrikes on multiple locations across Venezuela, including the capital city of [Caracas](55289.md), and captured and exfiltrated Venezuelan President Maduro. President Donald Trump announced that Maduro had been captured alongside his wife [Cilia Flores](9530834.md) and flown out of the country.[^cite_note-519] Attorney General [Pam Bondi](4550623.md) said both would face charges of [narcoterrorism](221350.md) in the United States.[^cite_note-NMC-520] The government formerly led by Maduro remained in place, and Venezuela's [Supreme Tribunal of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Tribunal_of_Justice_\(Venezuela\)) ordered Vice President [Delcy Rodríguez](44199228.md) to assume the presidency.[^cite_note-Reuters-01032026-521] In just a few months, Rodríguez reshuffled roughly 40% of her cabinet, replacing 17 ministers and updating military leadership to install her own loyalists. Powerful Maduro loyalists, such as long-serving Defense Minister ⁠[Vladimir Padrino López](45272341.md) and Attorney General [Tarek William Saab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarek_William_Saab), were stripped of their posts.[^cite_note-522][^cite_note-523] This consolidation was further highlighted when Venezuela’s Minister of Industry and National Production, [Alex Saab](61390840.md), was also captured in February 2026 in a joint operation by American and Venezuelan intelligence agencies.[^cite_note-Second_Arrest-524][^cite_note-Reuters_Second_Arrest-525] In May 2026, he was deported to the US.[^cite_note-526] Various analysts have called the US action "regime change."[^cite_note-527]

#### 2026: Cuba

Main article: [2026 Cuban crisis](82348646.md)

During its intervention in Venezuela, the US caused an oil shortage in Cuba with the aim to remove the current government by the end of the year.[^cite_note-528]

#### 2026: Iran

Main article: [2026 Iran war](82533582.md)

See also: [Regime change efforts in the 2026 Iran war](82756412.md)

In February 2026, the United States, alongside [Israel](9282173.md), launched a [major attack on Iran](82546969.md) with the stated goal of regime change.[^cite_note-529] On 28 February 2026, Iranian supreme leader [Ali Khamenei](385653.md) was [killed](82537558.md) in an Israeli missile strike.[^cite_note-530]

[^cite_note-190]: &#x20;Farouk–Sluglett, Marion; Sluglett, Peter (2001). *Iraq Since 1958: From Revolution to Dictatorship*. [I.B. Tauris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I.B._Tauris). p. 86. [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [9780857713735](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/9780857713735). Although individual leftists had been murdered intermittently over the previous years, the scale on which the killings and arrests took place in the spring and summer of 1963 indicates a closely coordinated campaign, and it is almost certain that those who carried out the raid on suspects' homes were working from lists supplied to them. Precisely how these lists had been compiled is a matter of conjecture, but it is certain that some of the Ba'th leaders were in touch with American intelligence networks, and it is also undeniable that a variety of different groups in Iraq and elsewhere in the Middle East had a strong vested interest in breaking what was probably the strongest and most popular communist party in the region.

[^cite_note-191]: &#x20;Gibson, Bryan R. (2015). *Sold Out? US Foreign Policy, Iraq, the Kurds, and the Cold War*. [Palgrave Macmillan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palgrave_Macmillan). p. 59. [^cite_note-192]: &#x20;Citino, Nathan J. (2017). "The People's Court". *Envisioning the Arab Future: Modernization in US–Arab Relations, 1945–1967*. [Cambridge University Press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambridge_University_Press). pp. 220–222. [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-1-108-10755-6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-1-108-10755-6).

[^cite_note-193]: &#x20;Wolfe-Hunnicutt, Brandon (March 2011). ["The End of the Concessionary Regime: Oil and American Power in Iraq, 1958-1972"](https://stacks.stanford.edu/file/druid\:tm772zz7352/Concessionary%20Regime%20%5Be-submit%5D-augmented.pdf) (PDF). pp. 84–85. One study from 1961 or 1962 included a section on "the capability of the U.S. Government to provide support to friendly groups, not in power, who are seeking the violent overthrow of a communist dominated and supported government." The study went on to discuss providing "covert assistance" to such groups and advised that, "Pinpointing of enemy concentrations and hideouts can permit effective use of 'Hunter‐Killer' teams." Given the Embassy's concern with the immediate suppression of Baghdad's sarifa population, it seems likely that American intelligence services would be interested in providing support to the Ba'thist "'Hunter‐Killer' teams."

[^cite_note-194]: &#x20;Wolfe-Hunnicutt, Brandon (2021). *The Paranoid Style in American Diplomacy: Oil and Arab Nationalism in Iraq*. [Stanford University Press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanford_University_Press). pp. 111–112. [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-1-5036-1382-9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-1-5036-1382-9). The CIA had long employed the method of targeted assassination in its global crusade against Communism. In 1954, a CIA team involved in the overthrow of Guatemalan leader Jacobo Arbenz compiled a veritable "Handbook of Assassination," replete with precise instructions for committing "political murder" and a list of suspected Guatemalan Communists to be targeted for "executive action." In the 1960s, the Kennedy administration made this rather ad hoc practice into a science. According to its special warfare doctrines, covertly armed and trained "Hunter-Killer teams" were a highly effective instrument in the root-and-branch eradication of Communist threats in developing nations. In what became known as the "Jakarta Method"—named for the systematic CIA-backed purge of Indonesian Communists in 1965—the CIA was involved in countless campaigns of mass murder in the name of anti-Communism.

[^cite_note-195]: &#x20;["CIA Covert Operations: The 1964 Overthrow of Cheddi Jagan in British Guiana | National Security Archive"](https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/briefing-book/intelligence/2020-04-06/cia-covert-operations-overthrow-cheddi-jagan-british-guiana-1964). *nsarchive.gwu.edu*. Retrieved October 2, 2024.

Despite Martens later denials of any such intent, these actions almost certainly aided in the death or detention of many innocent people. By 1984, \[bin Laden] was running a front organization known as [Maktab al-Khidamar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maktab_al-Khidamar) – the MAK – which funneled money, arms and fighters from the outside world into the Afghan war. What the CIA bio conveniently fails to specify (in its unclassified form, at least) is that the MAK was nurtured by Pakistan's state security services, the [Inter-Services Intelligence](2159021.md) agency, or ISI, the CIA's primary conduit for conducting the covert war against Moscow's occupation \[...] So bin Laden, along with a small group of Islamic militants from Egypt, Pakistan, Lebanon, Syria and Palestinian refugee camps all over the Middle East, became the 'reliable' partners of the CIA in its war against Moscow.

And some of the same warriors who fought the Soviets with the C.I.A.'s help are now fighting under Mr. bin Laden's banner.
